        Idea:
Design and implement a `moderation_decisions` table in the project's Supabase DB that tracks every time a Discord reaction is deleted/removed, with full details: reactor user_id/name, emoji, message_id/channel_id/guild_id, message author, message content snippet, removal timestamp, and a classification of WHO/WHAT removed it and WHY. Classifications should distinguish: (a) bot auto-removal for restricted emoji (flags/political/religious) via reactor_cog.py's restricted-emoji branch; (b) user self-removal (the reactor removed their own reaction); (c) other-user or moderator removal (someone else removed it, typically a mod); (d) bot "clear all" or message-delete cascades if distinguishable. Write paths to cover: (1) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` — the restricted-emoji auto-remove branch (must log BEFORE or alongside `message.remove_reaction` with classification='bot_auto_restricted' and reason=the specific category: flag/political/religious); (2) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` `on_raw_reaction_remove` — user-driven removals; compute classification by comparing `payload.user_id` to the reactor from the original add event (if available via discord_reaction_log). (3) Possibly the LoggerCog `_update_reaction` call path at `src/features/logging/logger_cog.py` if that's the right seam. Investigate the code first to pick the cleanest seam. Backfill consideration: there is an existing `discord_reaction_log` table that has `add` and `remove` events with emoji/user_id/message_id/guild_id/created_at — decide whether `moderation_decisions` is a new derived table or a view over that log, or a net-new write path with richer context that `discord_reaction_log` lacks. Bonus (include if cheap, skip if it expands scope): DM the ADMIN_USER_ID when a removal classified as 'suspicious' is logged — where suspicious = user removes their OWN prior 🤮 / 👎 / 😭 reaction (i.e. quietly retracting a negative reaction). Use the existing admin DM pattern from `src/common/base_bot.py:176-183`. Keep the implementation minimal and localized. Do NOT add feature flags, migrations framework, or new config surfaces beyond what's strictly required.

  "meta_commentary": "Implementation ordering is strict: migration SQL \\u2192 DB helpers \\u2192 ReactorCog marker infra + restricted branch \\u2192 `on_raw_reaction_remove` consumer \\u2192 clear/cascade listeners \\u2192 curator markers \\u2192 tests \\u2192 validate. Steps 3 and 6 both require `register_bot_removal` to exist; T6 depends on T3. Step 4 is the sole writer for the curator path \\u2014 do NOT add a `record_moderation_decision` call in curator.py itself. The restricted-emoji branch is the one exception that writes eagerly (it already holds live `user`/`message` objects and can short-circuit downstream event handling); its marker registration exists solely to suppress a duplicate write from the gateway `on_raw_reaction_remove` event. Key gotchas: (1) `_gate_check(guild_id)` will silently skip writes for out-of-scope guilds \\u2014 tests must set an allowed guild_id or patch the gate. (2) The marker key `(message_id, user_id, emoji_str)` must use the SAME emoji normalization at register time (curator + restricted branch) and consume time (`on_raw_reaction_remove`); mismatches silently leak user_self_removal rows. (3) `on_raw_message_delete` has a LoggerCog co-listener \\u2014 read the DB snapshot synchronously at the top of the ReactorCog handler. (4) Curator line numbers shift after the first insertion; find `message.remove_reaction('\\u274c', user)` fresh before each edit.         Idea:
Design and implement a `moderation_decisions` table in the project's Supabase DB that tracks every time a Discord reaction is deleted/removed, with full details: reactor user_id/name, emoji, message_id/channel_id/guild_id, message author, message content snippet, removal timestamp, and a classification of WHO/WHAT removed it and WHY. Classifications should distinguish: (a) bot auto-removal for restricted emoji (flags/political/religious) via reactor_cog.py's restricted-emoji branch; (b) user self-removal (the reactor removed their own reaction); (c) other-user or moderator removal (someone else removed it, typically a mod); (d) bot "clear all" or message-delete cascades if distinguishable. Write paths to cover: (1) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` — the restricted-emoji auto-remove branch (must log BEFORE or alongside `message.remove_reaction` with classification='bot_auto_restricted' and reason=the specific category: flag/political/religious); (2) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` `on_raw_reaction_remove` — user-driven removals; compute classification by comparing `payload.user_id` to the reactor from the original add event (if available via discord_reaction_log). (3) Possibly the LoggerCog `_update_reaction` call path at `src/features/logging/logger_cog.py` if that's the right seam. Investigate the code first to pick the cleanest seam. Backfill consideration: there is an existing `discord_reaction_log` table that has `add` and `remove` events with emoji/user_id/message_id/guild_id/created_at — decide whether `moderation_decisions` is a new derived table or a view over that log, or a net-new write path with richer context that `discord_reaction_log` lacks. Bonus (include if cheap, skip if it expands scope): DM the ADMIN_USER_ID when a removal classified as 'suspicious' is logged — where suspicious = user removes their OWN prior 🤮 / 👎 / 😭 reaction (i.e. quietly retracting a negative reaction). Use the existing admin DM pattern from `src/common/base_bot.py:176-183`. Keep the implementation minimal and localized. Do NOT add feature flags, migrations framework, or new config surfaces beyond what's strictly required.

        Idea:
Design and implement a `moderation_decisions` table in the project's Supabase DB that tracks every time a Discord reaction is deleted/removed, with full details: reactor user_id/name, emoji, message_id/channel_id/guild_id, message author, message content snippet, removal timestamp, and a classification of WHO/WHAT removed it and WHY. Classifications should distinguish: (a) bot auto-removal for restricted emoji (flags/political/religious) via reactor_cog.py's restricted-emoji branch; (b) user self-removal (the reactor removed their own reaction); (c) other-user or moderator removal (someone else removed it, typically a mod); (d) bot "clear all" or message-delete cascades if distinguishable. Write paths to cover: (1) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` — the restricted-emoji auto-remove branch (must log BEFORE or alongside `message.remove_reaction` with classification='bot_auto_restricted' and reason=the specific category: flag/political/religious); (2) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` `on_raw_reaction_remove` — user-driven removals; compute classification by comparing `payload.user_id` to the reactor from the original add event (if available via discord_reaction_log). (3) Possibly the LoggerCog `_update_reaction` call path at `src/features/logging/logger_cog.py` if that's the right seam. Investigate the code first to pick the cleanest seam. Backfill consideration: there is an existing `discord_reaction_log` table that has `add` and `remove` events with emoji/user_id/message_id/guild_id/created_at — decide whether `moderation_decisions` is a new derived table or a view over that log, or a net-new write path with richer context that `discord_reaction_log` lacks. Bonus (include if cheap, skip if it expands scope): DM the ADMIN_USER_ID when a removal classified as 'suspicious' is logged — where suspicious = user removes their OWN prior 🤮 / 👎 / 😭 reaction (i.e. quietly retracting a negative reaction). Use the existing admin DM pattern from `src/common/base_bot.py:176-183`. Keep the implementation minimal and localized. Do NOT add feature flags, migrations framework, or new config surfaces beyond what's strictly required.

        Idea:
Design and implement a `moderation_decisions` table in the project's Supabase DB that tracks every time a Discord reaction is deleted/removed, with full details: reactor user_id/name, emoji, message_id/channel_id/guild_id, message author, message content snippet, removal timestamp, and a classification of WHO/WHAT removed it and WHY. Classifications should distinguish: (a) bot auto-removal for restricted emoji (flags/political/religious) via reactor_cog.py's restricted-emoji branch; (b) user self-removal (the reactor removed their own reaction); (c) other-user or moderator removal (someone else removed it, typically a mod); (d) bot "clear all" or message-delete cascades if distinguishable. Write paths to cover: (1) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` — the restricted-emoji auto-remove branch (must log BEFORE or alongside `message.remove_reaction` with classification='bot_auto_restricted' and reason=the specific category: flag/political/religious); (2) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` `on_raw_reaction_remove` — user-driven removals; compute classification by comparing `payload.user_id` to the reactor from the original add event (if available via discord_reaction_log). (3) Possibly the LoggerCog `_update_reaction` call path at `src/features/logging/logger_cog.py` if that's the right seam. Investigate the code first to pick the cleanest seam. Backfill consideration: there is an existing `discord_reaction_log` table that has `add` and `remove` events with emoji/user_id/message_id/guild_id/created_at — decide whether `moderation_decisions` is a new derived table or a view over that log, or a net-new write path with richer context that `discord_reaction_log` lacks. Bonus (include if cheap, skip if it expands scope): DM the ADMIN_USER_ID when a removal classified as 'suspicious' is logged — where suspicious = user removes their OWN prior 🤮 / 👎 / 😭 reaction (i.e. quietly retracting a negative reaction). Use the existing admin DM pattern from `src/common/base_bot.py:176-183`. Keep the implementation minimal and localized. Do NOT add feature flags, migrations framework, or new config surfaces beyond what's strictly required.

        Idea:
Design and implement a `moderation_decisions` table in the project's Supabase DB that tracks every time a Discord reaction is deleted/removed, with full details: reactor user_id/name, emoji, message_id/channel_id/guild_id, message author, message content snippet, removal timestamp, and a classification of WHO/WHAT removed it and WHY. Classifications should distinguish: (a) bot auto-removal for restricted emoji (flags/political/religious) via reactor_cog.py's restricted-emoji branch; (b) user self-removal (the reactor removed their own reaction); (c) other-user or moderator removal (someone else removed it, typically a mod); (d) bot "clear all" or message-delete cascades if distinguishable. Write paths to cover: (1) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` — the restricted-emoji auto-remove branch (must log BEFORE or alongside `message.remove_reaction` with classification='bot_auto_restricted' and reason=the specific category: flag/political/religious); (2) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` `on_raw_reaction_remove` — user-driven removals; compute classification by comparing `payload.user_id` to the reactor from the original add event (if available via discord_reaction_log). (3) Possibly the LoggerCog `_update_reaction` call path at `src/features/logging/logger_cog.py` if that's the right seam. Investigate the code first to pick the cleanest seam. Backfill consideration: there is an existing `discord_reaction_log` table that has `add` and `remove` events with emoji/user_id/message_id/guild_id/created_at — decide whether `moderation_decisions` is a new derived table or a view over that log, or a net-new write path with richer context that `discord_reaction_log` lacks. Bonus (include if cheap, skip if it expands scope): DM the ADMIN_USER_ID when a removal classified as 'suspicious' is logged — where suspicious = user removes their OWN prior 🤮 / 👎 / 😭 reaction (i.e. quietly retracting a negative reaction). Use the existing admin DM pattern from `src/common/base_bot.py:176-183`. Keep the implementation minimal and localized. Do NOT add feature flags, migrations framework, or new config surfaces beyond what's strictly required.

    "The marker dict `_bot_initiated_removals` is in-memory only \u2014 bot restart between a bot-initiated remove and the gateway `on_raw_reaction_remove` event misclassifies the removal as `user_self_removal`. Accepted trade-off; TTL 60s bounds the window anyway.",

    "`on_raw_message_delete` also has a LoggerCog listener that soft-deletes the row.     "Clear-event payloads (`RawReactionClearEvent`, `RawReactionClearEmojiEvent`) have no actor field.         Idea:
Design and implement a `moderation_decisions` table in the project's Supabase DB that tracks every time a Discord reaction is deleted/removed, with full details: reactor user_id/name, emoji, message_id/channel_id/guild_id, message author, message content snippet, removal timestamp, and a classification of WHO/WHAT removed it and WHY. Classifications should distinguish: (a) bot auto-removal for restricted emoji (flags/political/religious) via reactor_cog.py's restricted-emoji branch; (b) user self-removal (the reactor removed their own reaction); (c) other-user or moderator removal (someone else removed it, typically a mod); (d) bot "clear all" or message-delete cascades if distinguishable. Write paths to cover: (1) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` — the restricted-emoji auto-remove branch (must log BEFORE or alongside `message.remove_reaction` with classification='bot_auto_restricted' and reason=the specific category: flag/political/religious); (2) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` `on_raw_reaction_remove` — user-driven removals; compute classification by comparing `payload.user_id` to the reactor from the original add event (if available via discord_reaction_log). (3) Possibly the LoggerCog `_update_reaction` call path at `src/features/logging/logger_cog.py` if that's the right seam. Investigate the code first to pick the cleanest seam. Backfill consideration: there is an existing `discord_reaction_log` table that has `add` and `remove` events with emoji/user_id/message_id/guild_id/created_at — decide whether `moderation_decisions` is a new derived table or a view over that log, or a net-new write path with richer context that `discord_reaction_log` lacks. Bonus (include if cheap, skip if it expands scope): DM the ADMIN_USER_ID when a removal classified as 'suspicious' is logged — where suspicious = user removes their OWN prior 🤮 / 👎 / 😭 reaction (i.e. quietly retracting a negative reaction). Use the existing admin DM pattern from `src/common/base_bot.py:176-183`. Keep the implementation minimal and localized. Do NOT add feature flags, migrations framework, or new config surfaces beyond what's strictly required.

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/banodoco-workspace/brain-of-bndc

        Idea:
Design and implement a `moderation_decisions` table in the project's Supabase DB that tracks every time a Discord reaction is deleted/removed, with full details: reactor user_id/name, emoji, message_id/channel_id/guild_id, message author, message content snippet, removal timestamp, and a classification of WHO/WHAT removed it and WHY. Classifications should distinguish: (a) bot auto-removal for restricted emoji (flags/political/religious) via reactor_cog.py's restricted-emoji branch; (b) user self-removal (the reactor removed their own reaction); (c) other-user or moderator removal (someone else removed it, typically a mod); (d) bot "clear all" or message-delete cascades if distinguishable. Write paths to cover: (1) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` — the restricted-emoji auto-remove branch (must log BEFORE or alongside `message.remove_reaction` with classification='bot_auto_restricted' and reason=the specific category: flag/political/religious); (2) `src/features/reacting/reactor_cog.py` `on_raw_reaction_remove` — user-driven removals; compute classification by comparing `payload.user_id` to the reactor from the original add event (if available via discord_reaction_log). (3) Possibly the LoggerCog `_update_reaction` call path at `src/features/logging/logger_cog.py` if that's the right seam. Investigate the code first to pick the cleanest seam. Backfill consideration: there is an existing `discord_reaction_log` table that has `add` and `remove` events with emoji/user_id/message_id/guild_id/created_at — decide whether `moderation_decisions` is a new derived table or a view over that log, or a net-new write path with richer context that `discord_reaction_log` lacks. Bonus (include if cheap, skip if it expands scope): DM the ADMIN_USER_ID when a removal classified as 'suspicious' is logged — where suspicious = user removes their OWN prior 🤮 / 👎 / 😭 reaction (i.e. quietly retracting a negative reaction). Use the existing admin DM pattern from `src/common/base_bot.py:176-183`. Keep the implementation minimal and localized. Do NOT add feature flags, migrations framework, or new config surfaces beyond what's strictly required.

    "Migration is STAGED only \u2014 inserts will fail (and be swallowed) until the SQL file is manually copied to the workspace `supabase/migrations/` repo and deployed. Deploy-order is migration-first.",
    "`_gate_check(guild_id)` silently skips writes for out-of-scope guilds \u2014 tests must pass an allowed `guild_id` or monkeypatch the gate, or assertions on `record_moderation_decision` calls will spuriously be zero.",
    "The marker dict `_bot_initiated_removals` is in-memory only \u2014 bot restart between a bot-initiated remove and the gateway `on_raw_reaction_remove` event misclassifies the removal as `user_self_removal`. Accepted trade-off; TTL 60s bounds the window anyway.",
    "Marker key is `(message_id, user_id, emoji_str)` \u2014 confirm `emoji_str` is normalized the same way at register time (curator + restricted branch) and at consume time (`on_raw_reaction_remove`). Mismatched unicode/custom-emoji strings would leak user_self_removal rows.",
    "Curator line numbers (296, 339, 460, 472) shift after the first insertion. Locate each `message.remove_reaction('\u274c', user)` freshly before each edit.",
    "`on_raw_message_delete` also has a LoggerCog listener that soft-deletes the row. ReactorCog's listener reads the snapshot synchronously at the top to avoid ordering races, but verify LoggerCog's soft-delete preserves `content`/`author_*` \u2014 if it ever nulls them, the snapshot read must run first.",
    "Clear-event payloads (`RawReactionClearEvent`, `RawReactionClearEmojiEvent`) have no actor field. Classifying as `moderator_cleared_*` is safe ONLY because no bot code calls `clear_reactions()`/`clear_reaction()` \u2014 if a future bot path adds that, it must pre-register markers to avoid misattribution.",
    "`get_active_reactors` relies on `discord_reactions.removed_at IS NULL` being accurate. If LoggerCog has not yet processed the add event when the clear/cascade fires, the enumeration will miss reactors. Accepted for v1.",
    "Admin DM is fire-and-forget \u2014 must not block the event handler or raise unhandled exceptions. Wrap the DM send in its own try/except.",
    "`is_suspicious` is ONLY set on `user_self_removal` of {\ud83e\udd2e, \ud83d\udc4e, \ud83d\ude2d}. Never on cascades, never on moderator clears, never on bot_auto_restricted/bot_curator_reject.",
    "Open minor flag (verifiability-0): Success criterion 12 requires dev-Discord manual smoke (flag reaction, admin reject, \ud83e\udd2e self-retract, moderator clear-all, message delete) \u2014 not automatable; document in PR body.",
    "No new config surfaces, env vars, or feature flags. ADMIN_USER_ID is reused as-is from base_bot.py.",
    "Content snippet is `message.content[:200]` \u2014 no sanitization beyond truncation. Truncate at every call site that writes a row.",
    "Debt watch: the task introduces no raw-SQL or RPC calls in `db_handler.py` \u2014 keep to the existing fluent-client pattern used by `add_reaction`/`remove_reaction`/`log_reaction_event` to avoid compounding the `db-handler-supabase-infrastructure` debt item."
  ],
